Instead lower the neighbour threshold n_ck >= 2 to >= 1 for W windows only, and log it (decided on DEV before freezing);\n- spot-check the _top_nb_W3 of 5 known concepts (e.g. optogenetics, microRNA, if they are in the frame) for sensible neighbours.\n\nT5 DEV-ONLY SANITY BEFORE THE FREEZE:\n- B5 alone reproduces the EXP5/EXP3 magnitude (DEV Spearman of the B5 ridge prediction with O2r_m50 about 0.7-0.83; report it).\n- The size diagnostic table.\n- The indicator correlation clusters show >= 6 distinct clusters at |rho| < 0.7 (families are really distinct; if not, say so).\n- Shuffled-outcome placebo on DEV: 20 permutations of O2r_resid within group.   "domain_practice": "WHAT I READ: the run's own prior artifacts on disk (EXP5 README, frozen_spec.json and scan_full.py; EXP3 features.py and config; EXP6 README and deviations; Eval1 F3 portability; the O5 dataset README); art_dxvRpQufMR0e's positioning summary (Guevara 2016 entry AUCs 0.68-0.90; the Weng 2013 precedent; the ANS template); and two targeted searches: Small, Boyack & Klavans 2014 (Research Policy 43:1450-1467), and the forecasting-evaluation literature (the Porter et al. 'Measuring tech emergence: A contest', TFSC 2020; the 2023 'What's next? Forecasting scientific research trends', Heliyon; the 2026 AI phase-transition early-warning preprint arXiv 2606.12828, which freezes thresholds on 2017-21 and scores 2023-25 with precision/recall against a 13.5% base rate). No domain handbook fits scientometrics, so the practice below rests on these sources and the run's measurements.\n\nBASELINES. Every emergence-indicator paper reports popularity/growth references: counts, growth, Kleinberg bursts (Kleinberg 2003), and the Porter/Carley emergence score, whose ingredients are novelty, persistence, growth and community. The first comparison a reviewer names is 'does it beat simple growth and volume?'. The 'Measuring tech emergence' contest and Maillart 2026 both show that growth alone explains much of the outcome. In diversification work the reviewer's first rival is relatedness density (Hidalgo 2007; Guevara 2016). Fair tuning means the baseline gets the same window, the same learner and the same CV as the candidate. Here that baseline is B5 (volume, growth, off-home share, entropy, reach) plus log_count/burst/accel/author growth as separate rows, fitted with the same L2 logistic or rank residualisation.\n\nDATA. Standard corpora are WoS/Scopus clusters (Small et al. 2014 use co-citation threads of about 14 papers per year), MAG/OpenAlex concepts (the arXiv 2606.03864 link forecasting), and topic-level OpenAlex. Legacy OpenAlex concepts (MAG FoS) are deprecated but still in the snapshot, and they are seeded from Wikipedia, which is a known selection condition. Paper-level topic classifiers used as discipline labels are circular for diffusion, which is why venue labels are used for features.\n\nCONTROLS. Hold the feature window fixed and separate it from the outcome window (a gap of 3 or more years). Hold the onset cohort fixed or control for it, because field growth and database coverage change by year. Hold field size fixed through rarefaction or residualisation. The confound most likely to catch this design is VOLUME: a structural indicator that relabels early size. The second is label coverage: Eval1 found all G-variant O1 gains were coverage artefacts.\n\nHOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Concept-level forecasting papers use hundreds to thousands of topics. Results with fewer than about 30 units per domain are dismissed (iteration 1's 47-concept panel was underpowered: rho_B5 = 0.83 left no room). Readers expect a CI, not a p-value alone, and per-domain results next to pooled ones. The emerging expectation is a temporal hold-out with frozen thresholds (arXiv 2606.12828). Effects already on record here are small (EXP5 H3 held-out partial rho about 0.03-0.07; EXP3 out-of-group psp for D_ratio 0.34 on 47 concepts, which will shrink). The held-out n here (742/1,113/1,352/165 plus a 4,356-concept cohort) gives an SE of about 0.017 pooled, so a partial rho of about 0.05 is detectable pooled. MATHDEC (SE about 0.08) can only confirm large effects. That is stated up front, not discovered.\n\nMEASURES AND REPORTING. For binary outcomes: AUC and delta-AUC over the baseline, or precision@k against the base rate. For continuous outcomes: Spearman or partial correlation. Report both global and per-field results, a random-effects pooled estimate with I2 (standard in this run and in meta-scientometrics), multiplicity correction, and a table of all indicators, not only the winners. Validation against EXTERNAL recognition (MeSH, Wikipedia, curated lists) is the gold standard that Rotolo et al. 2015 and Small et al. 2014 call for. It is rarely done because base rates are low and year-dependent.",
  "practice_alignment": "MEETS:\n(1) Growth/volume baselines are in every comparison: B5 is the conditioning set for every indicator, and popularity rows (count, burst, acceleration, author growth) compete as candidates. The learned models are compared against B5 with the SAME learner.\n(2) Feature and outcome windows are separated: features use t0..t0+2 only; outcomes use t0+6..t0+8 (O4: t0+3..t0+8). The EXP3 W3 window (t0+3..t0+4) is explicitly cut to avoid leakage.\n(3) Volume confounding is handled by rarefied O2r, residualised O2r_resid, a DEV size diagnostic per indicator, and partial correlations given B5.\n(4) Label coverage is a pre-declared sensitivity covariate set (the lesson of Eval1 D).\n(5) Domain hold-out plus temporal hold-out: 4 held-out home groups, plus a 2010-14 cohort split into DEV-home and non-DEV-home parts. This separates 'new time' from 'new domain'.\n(6) Per-group results, DL pooling with I2, sign counts, Holm within outcome families, concept-clustered refit bootstrap CIs, and the full portability table for all about 52 indicators. Domain-specific failures are reported, not averaged away, and one is pre-registered (P3).\n(7) External recognition (O5) is joined for the first time, with at-risk sets and onset-year controls for the Wikipedia wave.\n(8) The freeze and hash seal before scoring follow the frozen-threshold practice of recent early-warning work.\n\nDEPARTURES AND THEIR COST:\n(a) The held-out outcomes are not virgin. EXP5 already unsealed O1/O3/O2r for these concepts to test H1 (field retention) and H3 (G family). The ~45 other indicators were never scored on them, and no selection here touches held-out rows, so the practical leakage is limited to the G family, whose held-out rows are flagged 'previously scored'. Cost: a reader may call the held-out 'second-use'. It is disclosed in the README and in rq1_heldout.json.\n(b) Onset t0 = the first year with >= 20 TAG-grounded title matches (the EXP5 frame), not a Porter/Carley emergence score or co-citation threads. Concepts are legacy OpenAlex concepts, which carry a Wikipedia-seeding selection. This is justified because it is the run's single frozen frame, and changing it would break the 'one outcome table, one fold assignment' requirement. Cost: the results hold for named concepts, not for unnamed research fronts.\n(c) The ego windows are one year each (W1 = t0, W2 = t0+1, W3 = t0+2) instead of EXP3's 2+1+2 years, so they fit the 3-year feature window. Cost: noisier early ego statistics, especially for small concepts. It is mitigated by the T4 check and a DEV-decided threshold fallback, all before the freeze.\n(d) N_NULL = 200 and a betweenness cutoff, for compute reasons. Cost: small extra noise in D_z and a local, not global, betweenness. Both are logged as deviations.\n(e) The primary association measure is partial Spearman, not precision@k or AUC, for the continuous outcomes. This matches the direction and the run's iteration-2 practice. precision@top-decile is added to rq1_heldout.json for O2r_m50 and O5_WW so the result can be compared with the forecasting literature. It is cheap and closed inside the plan.\n(f) No Porter/Carley emergence-score baseline is computed. Its ingredients (novelty = newborn onset, persistence = O1-like, growth, community = author growth) are covered by family E rows, and a composite would add little. Cost: a reviewer may ask for it by name. This is noted as a limitation.\n(g) Venue labels come from each work's first source, and conference-heavy CS is under-covered (EXP5 deviation: base types article|review only). Cost: DEV CS indicators are measured on a thinner slice, which could make the pre-registered 'CS-only' results partly a coverage artefact. The label-coverage sensitivity addresses this.\n(h) O5 uses year_usable Wikipedia estimated dates and taxonomy intervals. The dates are coarse, and the Wikipedia creation wave of 2001-07 correlates with t0, so onset-year dummies enter the baseline. Cost: O5 is weaker as ground truth for early-onset concepts. This is reported with base rates by t0.\n(i) MATHDEC (165 concepts) is underpowered for effects below about 0.2, so its group-level result is reported as uninformative when its CI is wide, not as a failure."
}

It is the authoritative 12,499-row frame, and the concept list and fold assignment are taken from it unchanged.\\n- concept_outcomes.csv: O1 (binary, art_33 definition), O3, peak_year, N_outcome, O2r_m30, O2r_m50, O2_raw. These are the outcomes for every split, computed from the untruncated scan.\\n- concept_features_basic.csv: G, G_A, G_btw, G_deg, G_phimin, REL_home, RS, DOM_*, log_count, share, growth_ind, accel, burst, lab_entropy, lab_reach, lab_offhome_share, log_offhome_volume, and B5 = logvol, growth_c, offhome_share, entropy, reach. These are reused verbatim as families C and E and as the B5 baseline.\\n- scan/agg_counts.parquet (per concept, year, venue field, primary-topic field, tagstate and match type): the source of every per-field yearly state matrix, where grounded means tagstate == 1.\\n- scan/year_field_totals.npz; results/source_field.parquet (source -> venue-field lookup).\\n- Code: lexicon_v1.parquet with frozen_lexicon.sha256, matcher.py, common.py (surf_arrow, source_field_lut, works_files, GROUP_OF_FIELD), rangefile.py, scan_full.py (the process_file template), panel.py and frame.py (onset, home, early-window helpers), seal.py (the freeze/unseal gate pattern), models.py (Newton-IRLS logistic and the refit-bootstrap pattern).\\n- frozen_spec.json: the O2r_resid constants a = 4.790, b = -0.219. These are used only as a consistency check, because this plan refits them on DEV.\\n\\n(2) THE EGO-NETWORK MACHINERY: R/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_3/ (art_yrradSC27HtQ).\\n- features.py: concept_core(), which gives D_z/D_ratio/D_rare/D_sub, F_res/F_z, NOV/NOV_res, degree/strength/new-edge growth, edge persistence, turnover, participation, community transitions, ego density change, and betweenness/k-core/constraint of the concept inserted into the kNN backbone.\\n- common.py (lemmas, topic_lemma_df, lgC, slice_of).\\n- backbone/slice0-2.npz: comm (Leiden gamma 3), comm_q, deg, ka/kb kNN edges, a/b full edges, for the slices 2000-04, 2005-09 and 2010-14.\\n- scan/topic_ids.json (the topic index order), results/topic_meta.csv (subfield and field per topic), scan/ckpt.npz (background topic counts; used only to cross-check the new pass).\\n- results/exploratory_partial_association.json and screen_result.json: the iteration-1 P78 values, reported as the prior.\\n\\n(3) THE D3 DEFINITIONS AND THE PHI BACKBONE: R/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_6/ (art_N-mpomDZZ1ln).\\n- lib/h2.py: states(), which gives ENTERED/RETAINED/LOST verbatim.\\n- lib/stats_core.py: DL pooling and bootstrap helpers.\\n- inputs/field_backbone.json: the frozen 1998-2002 26-field PMI phi.\\n- results/frame_concepts.csv: the Exp6 concept IDs, used only for the overlap sensitivity.\\n- README pass-2 evidence: reading id/title/referenced_works/authorships over all 2,040 files took 12.7 min, which sizes Pass A and Pass B.\\n\\n(4) THE PRE-REGISTERED PRIOR: R/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_evaluation_1/eval_out.json, key F3_exp3_portability (within-group rho of every EXP3 indicator on the 4 DEV groups of P78). It is the source of the pre-registered predictions.\\n\\n(5) THE O5 GROUND TRUTH, the declared dependency art_O7Dq4L02QnDN: R/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_2/full_data_out/full_data_out_{1,2,3}.json. It has the concept_recognition records (openalex_id, qid, qid_resolved, events[] with source, event_type, year, year_usable, relation, match_confidence) and the external_entries_* parts. See also its README.md and out/coverage_report.json.\\n\\nNEGATIVE FINDINGS BUILT PAST, and NOT re-tested as headlines:\\n- the gateway retention lead (EXP5 H1 dAUC -0.00001; Eval1 union +0.001);\\n- G-variant O1 gains being label-coverage artefacts (Eval1 D), which is why label_coverage_early enters every sensitivity baseline;\\n- A*_h failing on P78;\\n- D_z failing the size diagnostic (EXP3), which makes D_ratio the primary D;\\n- CS-only degree/strength/new-edge growth (EXP3), now a pre-registered held-out negative.\\n\\nG, G_A and G_btw were ALREADY scored once on these held-out concepts for O2r_resid (EXP5 H3). They stay in the matrix, but their held-out rows are labelled 'previously scored', not confirmatory.\",\n  \"implementation_pseudocode\": \"# ======================= 0. SETUP (about 15 min) =======================\\n# Read the skills aii-python, aii-parallel-computing, aii-long-running-tasks, aii-use-hardware and aii-json first.\\n# WS = this workspace; R = the run root. Every write goes under WS.\\nuv venv; uv pip install numpy pandas pyarrow scipy scikit-learn statsmodels python-igraph leidenalg interpret loguru matplotlib pyahocorasick nltk joblib\\n# Copy (do not import across workspaces) into WS/lib/ and record the sha256 of each copy in WS/results/provenance.json:\\n#   EXP5 matcher.py, rangefile.py, common.py (rename to common5.py), lexicon_v1.parquet (check the sha against frozen_lexicon.sha256),\\n#        results/source_field.parquet, snapshot/works_manifest.json\\n#   EXP3 features.py (rename to ego.py), common.py (rename to common3.py), backbone/slice*.npz, scan/topic_ids.json,\\n#        results/topic_meta.csv\\n#   EXP6 lib/h2.py, lib/stats_core.py, inputs/field_backbone.json\\n# If R is not mounted: re-download the manifests with EXP5's restore logic (snapshot/works_manifest.json lists the 2,040 S3\\n#   keys; public, zero credit), rebuild the frame from the definitions, and log everything in WS/results/deviations.json.\\nframe = read_csv(EXP5/frame_concepts.csv)                       # 12,499 rows\\nassert counts by split/group == {DEV 4771, PHYS 742, LIFEENV 1113, SOC 1352, MATHDEC 165, COHORT 4356}\\nframe['cohort_part'] = where(split == COHORT, 'COH_DEVHOME' if group in {CS, Eng, BGM, Med} else 'COH_OTHER', NA)\\nframe['in_exp6'] = concept_id in EXP6/results/frame_concepts.csv   # report the overlap count\\n\\n# ======================= 1. PASS A: grounded frame matches with work/topic/author IDs =======================\\n# One zero-credit pass over all 2,040 files, adapted from EXP5 scan_full.process_file. It runs in the BACKGROUND\\n# (PID-managed, resumable per file) while steps 3-4 are coded.\\nCOLS_A = EXP5.COLS + ['id', 'topics.list.element.id', 'authorships.list.element.author.id']\\n# EXP5.COLS already has title, year, type, is_paratext, is_xpac, source.id, topics field id, primary_topic field id,\\n# concepts id/score. There is no referenced_works here: Pass B handles citations.\\nper file fi:\\n   tb = read_columns(key, size, COLS_A, n_threads=8)\\n   base, vfield, tagstate: computed EXACTLY as in EXP5 (same automaton built from the FULL lexicon_v1, same TAG_MIN = 0.3)\\n   (a) BG[year, topic] += base works per topic per year, and GT[year] += base works with >= 1 topic\\n       (a full-corpus background for 1995-2022; it extends EXP3's ckpt)\\n   (b) h = verified hits with tagstate == 1 AND ci in the frame set.\\n       COUNTS[ci, year, vfield] += 1 for all years  -> used by the reproduction check\\n   (c) for hits with t0[ci] - 3 <= year <= t0[ci] + 2: emit a row\\n       (ci, year, work_id int64, vfield, topic_idx list[int16] via topic_ids.json, author_ids list[int64] only if year >= t0)\\n   (d) RSAMPLE: base works with year 2003..2016 and splitmix64(fi, row) % 400 == 0 (about 0.25%, about 250k works):\\n       emit (work_id, year, vfield). This is the citation reference set for normalising O4.\\n   write the parts to WS/passA/parts/*.parquet and done_{fi}.json\\nmerge -> WS/data/frame_matches_early.parquet (split into parts < 90 MB), bg_topics.npz, counts_check.parquet, ref_sample.parquet\\n# Expected size: about 12.5k concepts x about 150 early works = about 2M rows; topics about 3 ids, authors about 5 ids\\n# -> roughly 100-200 MB of parquet.\\nCHECK A1 (STOP rule): for every frame concept, Spearman over years of COUNTS[ci, year, :].sum() against grounded counts from\\n   EXP5 agg_counts (tagstate == 1). The median must be >= 0.99 AND recomputed early_volume must equal frame.early_volume for\\n   >= 99% of concepts. Otherwise STOP, log the mismatch and diagnose (automaton, lexicon hash, base filter) before any feature.\\nCHECK A2: for the years both cover, BG must reproduce EXP3 ckpt bg with Spearman >= 0.999. Unknown topic ids are counted and logged.\\n\\n# ======================= 2. PASS B: citations received by early works (O4) =======================\\ntargets = sorted int64 array of (early work ids, i.e. year in t0..t0+2 of frame concepts) UNION ref_sample ids\\nCOLS_B = ['publication_year', 'type', 'is_paratext', 'is_xpac', 'referenced_works']\\nper file: citing = base works (same filter) with year 2003..2022; flatten referenced_works -> int64 via utf8_slice at 'W';\\n   m = isin(refs, targets) (np.searchsorted on the sorted targets)\\n   CIT[target_id, citing_year] += 1 (sparse; emit (target_id, citing_year, n))\\nmerge -> WS/data/cites_early.parquet   # EXP6 read referenced_works for all files in 12.7 min\\n# If Pass B has not finished by T+3h15m: drop O4, log it, continue.\\n\\n# ======================= 3. FEATURE MATRIX (window t0..t0+2 ONLY) =======================\\n# Per-concept yearly per-field grounded counts n_cj(t) come from EXP5 agg_counts (tagstate == 1, vfield > 0 = labelled).\\n# home = frame.home (a field code; for intersection-born, parse all home codes the frame records).\\nFAMILY E  (reference / popularity; 'E' rows): log_count, share, growth_ind, accel, burst, logvol, growth_c\\n   (from concept_features_basic)\\n   + author_growth = log(1 + distinct authors t0+2) - log(1 + distinct authors t0)                         (Pass A)\\n   + n_authors_early = log(1 + distinct authors t0..t0+2)\\n   B5 = [logvol, growth_c, offhome_share, entropy, reach]  # identical to iterations 1-2; B5 is the baseline, not a candidate\\nFAMILY F  (disciplinary): lab_entropy, lab_reach, lab_offhome_share, log_offhome_volume (reuse)\\n   + rao_stirling = sum_{i != j} p_i p_j (1 - phi_n[i, j]), with phi_n = phi rescaled to [0, 1]; labelled works t0..t0+2\\n   + fields_gained_per_yr = (|ENTERED(t0+2)| - |ENTERED(t0)|) / 2\\nFAMILY G  (landing): G, G_A, G_btw, G_deg, G_phimin, REL_home, RS (reuse; flag 'previously_scored_heldout')\\nFAMILY FR (retained frontier, D3 verbatim via h2.states() on n_cj(t) restricted to t0..t0+2):\\n   CONTACT_REACH = #off-home fields with >= 1 labelled paper in t0..t0+2\\n   RETAINED_REACH = #off-home fields with >= 2 papers in at least 2 of the 3 years\\n   RETENTION_RATIO_early = RETAINED_REACH / max(CONTACT_REACH, 1)  (NA if CONTACT_REACH = 0 -> 0 plus a missing flag)\\n   FRONTIER_POTENTIAL = sum over k not in ENTERED(t0+2), k != home, of mean_{j in early-retained} phi[j, k]\\n                        (0 if there are no early-retained fields)\\n   also D_rca_end, D_vol_end, M0_density_end at t0+2 (the D3 formulas), kept for the portability table only\\nFAMILY A  (co-occurrence ego network, art_yrradSC27HtQ features.concept_core adapted):\\n   works = [(year, topic_idx tuple)] from frame_matches_early for t0-3..t0+2\\n   windows: PRE = t0-3..t0-1, W1 = {t0}, W2 = {t0+1}, W3 = {t0+2}   # DEVIATION: the EXP3 W3 = t0+3..t0+4 would leak past the window\\n   new_edge_rate = (M / 3) / (n1 + 1)                                # the window is 3 years, not 5\\n   background: bg_window() from Pass A's BG/GT (not EXP3's ckpt, whose year range may stop early)\\n   SELF topics: the EXP3 rule, with the concept name plus aliases_used from the frame\\n   slice_of(): the EXP3 SLICES (2000-04, 05-09, 10-14); years 2015-16 clamp to slice 2 (log it)\\n   outputs kept (about 26): D_z, D_ratio, D_rare, D_sub, D_obs(=M new neighbours), NOV, NOV_res, F_res, F_z,\\n     deg_W1, deg_W3, deg_growth, str_growth, new_edge_rate, edge_persistence, turnover, participation, n_comm_W3,\\n     comm_entropy (Shannon entropy of W3 neighbour-community weights; new, 3 lines), comm_transitions,\\n     ego_density_W3, ego_density_change, btw_t4 (rename btw_end), btw_change, kcore_end, constraint_end, constraint_change\\n   dropped as near-duplicate variants: D_lag, D_q, D_withself, F_bg (logged)\\n   SPEED: N_NULL = 200 (EXP3 used 200 for its reliability splits); betweenness with cutoff = 4 on the kNN backbone.\\n   Timing test on 60 random concepts first (see testing_plan); extrapolate over 7 workers x 4 vCPU.\\n   If the projection exceeds 100 min: keep ALL held-out-group concepts (3,372) and a stratified random 2,000 DEV plus\\n   2,000 COHORT (seed 20260928, stratified by group x t0), and log it.\\n   Parallelism: ProcessPoolExecutor(spawn, 4 workers), with the context loaded once per worker as in EXP3.\\nFAMILY S  (social; candidate S, Cheng-style): for off-home labelled early works (vfield not home, t0..t0+2):\\n   build a graph with authors as nodes and a clique per paper; S_comp = #connected components / #off-home papers;\\n   S_comp_n = #components / #distinct off-home authors; also S_isolated_share.\\n   If author ids are missing for > 50% of those works, compute S only where coverage >= 50% and log coverage.\\n   If the id column is unreadable, drop family S and log it.\\nOUTPUT: WS/results/indicator_matrix.parquet (ci x about 52 indicators + family tags) and indicator_dictionary.csv\\n   (name, family, window, formula, source file, expected sign from F3 where it exists, pre-registered prediction flag).\\nDIAGNOSTICS on DEV only: missing rates; Spearman of each indicator with logvol and growth_c (the size diagnostic,\\n   flag |rho| > 0.6); the indicator x indicator Spearman matrix with average-linkage clustering on 1 - |rho|\\n   -> results/indicator_corr_dev.csv and figures/indicator_clusters.png.\\n\\n# ======================= 4. OUTCOMES (one table, one fold assignment) =======================\\nO1c  = log(1 + N_grounded t0+6..t0+8) - log(1 + N_grounded t0..t0+2)   (continuous; the direction's definition; from agg_counts)\\nO1b  = concept_outcomes.O1 (EXP5 binary sustained-share rule; secondary, AUC)\\nO2r_m50 (primary breadth), O2r_m30 = concept_outcomes (exact hypergeometric, no truncation)\\nO2r_resid = O2r_m50 - (a + b * logvol), with a and b fitted by OLS on DEV ONLY, frozen and reported next to EXP5's constants\\nO3   = concept_outcomes.O3 (the art_33 transience rule verbatim; report base rates per group)\\nO4   = log((1 + C_late / 6) / (1 + C_early / 3)) summed over the concept's early works, where C_early = citations in\\n       t0..t0+2 and C_late = citations in t0+3..t0+8 (cites_early),\\n       MINUS the same quantity for the ref_sample works matched on (publication year, venue field), taking the\\n       expectation over the concept's early-work mix of year x field. This is field-and-year normalised\\n       (an MNCS-style expected value).\\nO5   (art_O7Dq4L02QnDN): join on concept_id == int(openalex_id.strip('C')); fall back to qid_resolved; report the join rate.\\n     Events used: year_usable == True AND relation == 'same', from the sources MeSH (DateIntroduced), Wikipedia creation\\n     (exact or calibrated estimate), Wikidata P571/P575, taxonomy_added_between (ACM CCS, MSC, PACS/PhySH), and curated\\n     lists EXCEPT Clarivate/CAS Research Fronts, which are citation-derived and circular with O4.\\n     AT RISK: no qualifying event with year < t0 and no mesh_baseline flag. Concepts not at risk are set to NA for O5.\\n     O5 = 1 if any qualifying event has t0 <= year <= t0+8 (MeSH and taxonomy require year > t0).\\n     O5_WW = the same, using Wikipedia and Wikidata only; defined for every group.\\n     Report the base rate and the at-risk n per group. A group with < 20 positives is DROPPED from O5 scoring (O5_WW too).\\n     Because the Wikipedia creation wave is 2001-07, the O5/O5_WW baselines also include onset-year dummies (B5+t0).\\n     Sensitivity: relation in {same, narrower}.\\nOUTCOME SEAL: write WS/data/outcomes_dev.parquet (DEV rows) and WS/data/outcomes_sealed.parquet (HELDOUT + COHORT rows).\\n   Log the sha256 of the sealed file. lib/seal.py load_heldout() raises unless results/frozen_spec.json exists and its\\n   hash matches logs/seal.log, and it refuses a second call (the EXP5 seal.py pattern).\\n   NOTE in the README: EXP5 already unsealed O1/O3/O2r for these concepts for its H1/H3 tests. Held-out is fresh for\\n   every indicator EXCEPT the G family.\\n\\n# ======================= 5. DEV SELECTION (DEV rows only) =======================\\nContinuous outcomes {O1c, O2r_m50, O2r_resid, O4}:\\n   psp(x, y | B5) = Pearson( resid(rank x ~ rank B5 + group dummies + t0 dummies), resid(rank y ~ same) )\\n   CI: 1,000 concept-bootstrap resamples; the residualisation is REFITTED in each resample (the concept is the unit).\\nBinary outcomes {O1b, O3, O5, O5_WW}:\\n   dAUC = AUC(B5 + x) - AUC(B5), for L2 logistic models evaluated leave-one-DEV-group-out (4 folds by home group) with\\n   out-of-fold predictions; x is standardised on the training folds. CI: 1,000 concept-bootstrap resamples refitting both\\n   models (a vectorised Newton-IRLS from EXP5 models.py).\\nPer outcome, frozen ranking rule:\\n   eligible = indicators with DEV CI excluding 0 and missing <= 30%\\n   order by |estimate| descending; greedy de-duplication: skip x if |Spearman(x, already selected)| > 0.85 on DEV\\n   top10[outcome] = the first 10; if fewer than 10 are eligible, fill by |estimate| (flag 'filled')\\n   store the frozen SIGN of each selected indicator\\nunion_top10 = the 10 indicators with the best mean rank across the 8 outcomes (same de-duplication)\\nSensitivity rankings (reported, not used to freeze): given B5 + label_coverage_early + tag_coverage + precision_c;\\n   and ranking by AUC of top vs bottom tercile within group.\\nLEARNED MODELS (all DEV, hyperparameters by leave-one-DEV-group-out CV):\\n   continuous: ElasticNetCV(l1_ratio in {0.1, 0.5, 0.9, 1.0}) on standardised indicators + B5;\\n     ExplainableBoostingRegressor(interactions=10, outer_bags=8, random_state=20260928)\\n   binary: LogisticRegressionCV(penalty='l1', solver='saga', Cs=20);\\n     ExplainableBoostingClassifier(interactions=10, outer_bags=8)\\n   imputation: DEV medians plus missing flags for indicators with > 5% missing; the scaler is frozen\\n   saved: learned_model.json (coefficients, the L1 path over C, EBM term importances, shape-function tables for the\\n     top 8 terms, pairwise terms), models/*.joblib\\nPOWER (DEV simulation): plant partial rho = 0.05 / 0.10 in the held-out group sizes; report P(CI > 0) per group and pooled.\\n   Expected SE of partial rho: about 0.037 (PHYS), 0.03 (LIFEENV), 0.027 (SOC), 0.078 (MATHDEC), 0.017 (pooled).\\n   Store these as MDE = 2.8 x SE in frozen_spec.\\nFREEZE: results/frozen_spec.json = {indicator list and formulas, window definitions, N_NULL, B5, covariate sets,\\n   the O2r_resid a/b, the O5 rules, top10 per outcome with signs, union_top10, learner hyperparameters, scaler constants,\\n   imputation medians, bootstrap B and seed, Holm families, DL method, pre-registered predictions P1-P5 (below),\\n   sha256 of every lib/*.py and of indicator_matrix.parquet}\\n   -> write sha256(frozen_spec.json) to logs/seal.log; git-commit the code; ONLY THEN call load_heldout() (once).\\n\\n# ======================= 6. HELD-OUT SCORING (once) =======================\\nunits = [PHYS, LIFEENV, SOC, MATHDEC, COH_DEVHOME, COH_OTHER]\\nfor each outcome, for each indicator in top10[outcome] (and union_top10), for each unit:\\n   continuous: psp given B5 + t0 dummies within the unit; concept bootstrap, B = 1,000\\n   binary: dAUC using the FROZEN DEV coefficients (B5 model and B5 + x model) applied to the unit. CI: joint refit\\n     bootstrap (resample DEV concepts -> refit -> resample unit concepts -> score), B = 1,000\\n   DL-pool the 4 held-out groups (Fisher z for rho; dAUC with bootstrap SE) -> pooled estimate, 95% CI, tau2, I2\\n   sign agreement with the frozen sign: count over 4 groups + 2 cohort parts; exact sign test p\\nHolm within each outcome family (the 10 pooled tests of that outcome's top 10), using p from the pooled z.\\nLEARNED vs SINGLE vs B5 on the SAME held-out units:\\n   continuous: Spearman(pred, y) and out-of-sample R2 of B5-only ElasticNet vs ElasticNet-all vs EBM vs B5 + best single\\n     (the best single is the DEV #1 indicator, frozen); paired concept bootstrap for the differences\\n   binary: AUC, delta-AUC, Brier score and calibration slope for the same four models\\nPORTABILITY TABLE (all about 52 indicators, x 4 DEV groups + 4 held-out groups + 2 cohort parts): psp with O2r_m50, O2r_resid\\n   and O1c, plus the raw Spearman with O2r_m50. Rows outside the top 10 are labelled EXPLORATORY.\\n   -> portability_table.csv (indicator, family, unit, outcome, n, rho, ci_lo, ci_hi, frozen/exploratory,\\n      previously_scored flag)\\nPRE-REGISTERED PREDICTIONS (frozen before the unseal; evaluated on the pooled held-out groups):\\n   P1 entropy, D_rare, D_ratio, participation and NOV_res: raw Spearman with O2r_m50 > 0 (CI > 0) in >= 3 of 4 groups;\\n      AND for D_rare, D_ratio, participation and NOV_res: pooled psp | B5 CI upper bound < 0.10 ('add little').\\n      Entropy is in B5, so only its raw part is testable.\\n   P2 edge_persistence: pooled raw rho and psp with O2r_m50 both < 0.\\n   P3 deg_growth, str_growth, new_edge_rate: FAIL held-out = pooled psp CI includes 0 OR a sign flip in >= 2 of 4 groups.\\n      Also report their CS-only DEV values.\\n   P4 RETENTION_RATIO_early and FRONTIER_POTENTIAL: pooled psp > 0 with CI > 0 for O2r_resid AND O1c.\\n   P5 CONTACT_REACH: pooled psp CI includes 0. Because B5 contains reach, ALSO report P4/P5 given B5-minus-reach, so the\\n      test is not trivial by construction.\\n   Each prediction gets a verdict (HOLDS / FAILS / UNTESTABLE) in rq1_heldout.json.\\nSENSITIVITIES (post-seal, labelled): excluding in_exp6 concepts; excluding intersection-born; label-coverage covariates\\n   added; O5 relation in {same, narrower}; O2r_m30.\\n\\n# ======================= 7. EXPLAIN, FIGURES, OUTPUTS =======================\\nfigures/portability_heatmap.png (+pdf): indicator (rows, grouped by family) x unit; colour = psp with O2r_m50; a dot where\\n   the CI excludes 0; frozen top-10 rows in bold\\nfigures/heldout_forest_<outcome>.png: per-unit and DL-pooled estimates with CIs for the top 10\\nfigures/learned_vs_single.png: bars of held-out delta-AUC / delta-Spearman with CIs for the models, the best single and B5\\nfigures/ebm_shapes.png: the top 6 EBM shape functions for O2r_resid and O5_WW\\nfigures/indicator_clusters.png (DEV correlation dendrogram)\\nfigures/o5_base_rates.png\\nCase exemplars: for the best portable indicator, list the 3 held-out concepts with the highest and the lowest predicted\\n   O2r_resid, with their top-10 W3 ego neighbours (features.py _top_nb_W3) -> results/case_exemplars.json\\nWrite: indicator_matrix.parquet, outcomes.parquet (merged after the unseal), rq1_dev_selection.json, frozen_spec.json\\n   (+ logs/seal.log), rq1_heldout.json, portability_table.csv, learned_model.json, deviations.json, provenance.json,\\n   method_out.json (exp_gen_sol_out: one example per concept with input = the indicator vector, output = outcomes,\\n   predict_B5 / predict_best_single / predict_EBM for every outcome; DEV rows are out-of-fold)\\n   + full/mini/preview variants (aii-json; split with aii-file-size-limit if > limit).\\nREADME.md (layout, how to run, results table, 'Restoring removed files') and .aii/manifest.yaml:\\n   keep: data/frame_matches_early*.parquet, cites_early.parquet, bg_topics.npz, results/, figures/, models/;\\n   delete: .venv/ (regenerable: uv venv + uv pip install -r requirements.txt); passA/parts/ and passB/parts/\\n   (regenerable: uv run passA.py / passB.py); __pycache__/.\",\n  \"fallback_plan\": \"F1 SNAPSHOT UNREACHABLE OR TOO SLOW for Pass A (projected > 90 min after 40 files):\\n(a) First reduce COLS_A: drop author ids, which drops family S (log it).\\n(b) If still too slow, process a deterministic 50% of files (every even file index). This is a random subsample of works, so ego counts are halved. Mark it in deviations and keep the EXP5 agg_counts reproduction check on the sampled files only (expected ratio about 0.5).\\n(c) If the S3 snapshot is entirely unreachable: family A cannot be computed. Deliver the RQ1 matrix from families E, F, G and FR and B5 (about 26 indicators, all computable from agg_counts and concept_features_basic). Add a coarse FIELD-level co-occurrence ego proxy from agg_counts ptfield (the primary-topic field of each grounded work) against the 26-field co_by_year background: new-field-neighbour count, persistence, participation over the phi-Leiden communities of the 26-field backbone. State plainly that the fine topic-level ego network was not recomputed.\\n\\nF2 CHECK A1 FAILS (median Spearman < 0.99 against agg_counts). Diagnose in this order: lexicon sha, automaton built from the full lexicon, base-type filter, TAG_MIN, venue lookup. Fix and rerun on 5 files before continuing. Never proceed with an unreproduced frame.\\n\\nF3 PASS B DOES NOT FIT (not finished by T+3h15m, or targets > 5M ids): drop O4 and log it. As a secondary substitute, use the snapshot's present-day cited_by_count of early works, normalised by the ref-sample year x field mean. That column is cheap and could be added to Pass A as 'cited_by_count'; mark it as NOT windowed. It has no role in selection. If both are missing, O4 is dropped from all tables.\\n\\nF4 EGO FEATURES TOO SLOW (timing test > 100 min projected): apply in order\\n(i) N_NULL 200 -> 100;\\n(ii) betweenness cutoff 4 -> 3, or replace it with the degree of the inserted node in the kNN graph;\\n(iii) the stratified subsample (all held-out concepts, 2,000 DEV, 2,000 COHORT).\\nEach step is logged; held-out groups are never subsampled.\\n\\nF5 O5 SPARSE: if fewer than 2 held-out groups have >= 20 positives for O5, the taxonomy/MeSH O5 is reported descriptively only, and O5_WW becomes the only confirmatory recognition outcome. If O5_WW is also < 20 in a group, that group is dropped from O5 scoring and the base rates are reported. If the join rate is < 90%, try the qid_resolved join, then report the unmatched concepts.\\n\\nF6 DEGENERATE BINARY OUTCOMES: O3 or O1b with < 20 positives in a group means that group is dropped for that outcome (O3 was all-Medicine in iteration 1; check the base rates first).\\n\\nF7 EBM / interpret INSTALL FAILS: use sklearn HistGradientBoosting with monotone-free settings plus permutation importance and 1-D partial dependence as the interpretable substitute, and log it.\\n\\nF8 TIME PRESSURE at T+4h45m: skip the sensitivities and case exemplars. The sealed held-out scoring of the top 10, the learned-vs-single comparison, the portability table, the prediction verdicts, the README and the manifest are mandatory and come first.\\n\\nF9 AN INDICATOR IS ALL-NaN IN A HELD-OUT GROUP (e.g. ego features need M >= 3): report n and mark it UNTESTABLE for that group. Never impute outcomes, and never impute features on held-out with anything but the frozen DEV medians.\",\n  \"testing_plan\": \"T0 UNIT TESTS (no network, < 5 min; results/unit_tests.json).\\n(1) h2.states() on a hand-made 5-field x 6-year count matrix: ENTERED/RETAINED/LOST are correct, including the '>= 2 years earlier' retained rule and the off-home restriction.\\n(2) RETAINED_REACH and FRONTIER_POTENTIAL on a toy phi (3 fields) against hand values.\\n(3) Partial Spearman equals the Pearson of rank residuals; on data where y = f(B5) + noise with x independent, the mean psp over 200 simulations is within 0.01 of 0.\\n(4) The dAUC pipeline with a planted effect (x adds 0.5 SD log-odds) returns dAUC > 0.02; with shuffled x, |mean| < 0.005.\\n(5) The DL pooling routine reproduces a textbook example (EXP6 stats_core, or metafor's dat.bcg log RR: tau2 = 0.313).\\n(6) The seal gate raises if frozen_spec is missing and raises on a second unseal.\\n(7) The O5 rule on 4 synthetic concepts: an event before t0 makes the concept not at risk; t0+8 is inclusive; Research Fronts are ignored.\\n(8) Ego features on EXP3's P78 dev concepts with the ORIGINAL EXP3 windows reproduce EXP3 results/features*.csv (D_ratio, participation, NOV_res; Spearman >= 0.95 after title-match -> TAG differences). This validates the port before the window change.\\n\\nT1 PASS A MINI (3 files: take indices 65, 1125 and 1407, which EXP5 used for its stage tests).\\n- Runtime per file gives an extrapolation over 2,040 files at 4 workers. Proceed if <= 70 min, else fallback F1(a).\\n- The per-file hit counts for frame concepts with tagstate == 1 must EXACTLY equal the counts implied by EXP5 scan/stage_test_parts/agg_{fi}.npz (decode uC keys: ci, year, vfield, ptfield, tagstate, mt).\\n- Topic id mapping: < 1% unknown ids.\\n\\nT2 PASS A FULL: check A1 (median Spearman >= 0.99; early_volume equal for >= 99%) and check A2 (background Spearman >= 0.999 against EXP3 ckpt). Report the numbers in results/checks.json.\\n\\nT3 PASS B MINI (3 files): some early works receive citations; citing years are >= the cited year for > 99.5% of links; the per-file runtime extrapolates to <= 25 min.\\n\\nT4 EGO TIMING AND SANITY on 60 random DEV concepts:\\n- time per concept, projected to the full frame;\\n- no all-NaN column;\\n- M (new neighbours) has median > 3; if the median is <= 3 with 1-year windows, switch to W1 = t0..t0+1, W2 = t0+1..t0+2 is NOT allowed (overlap). Instead lower the neighbour threshold n_ck >= 2 to >= 1 for W windows only, and log it (decided on DEV before freezing);\\n- spot-check the _top_nb_W3 of 5 known concepts (e.g. optogenetics, microRNA, if they are in the frame) for sensible neighbours.\\n\\nT5 DEV-ONLY SANITY BEFORE THE FREEZE:\\n- B5 alone reproduces the EXP5/EXP3 magnitude (DEV Spearman of the B5 ridge prediction with O2r_m50 about 0.7-0.83; report it).\\n- The size diagnostic table.\\n- The indicator correlation clusters show >= 6 distinct clusters at |rho| < 0.7 (families are really distinct; if not, say so).\\n- Shuffled-outcome placebo on DEV: 20 permutations of O2r_resid within group. The number of indicators with DEV CI excluding 0 must be about 5% of about 52 (<= 6 on average); otherwise the bootstrap is anticonservative, so fix it before freezing.\\n- Learned-model LOGO-CV on DEV beats B5 by a stated margin, or this is reported as a null.\\n\\nT6 PRE-UNSEAL CHECKLIST (logs/seal.log):\\n- no held-out/cohort outcome column is present in any object built before the freeze (grep the pickles/parquets written before the freeze for the sealed column names on held-out ci);\\n- git commit hash;\\n- frozen_spec sha.\\n\\nT7 POST-SCORING AUDIT (independent code path, audit.py):\\n- recompute the pooled psp for the top 3 indicators of O2r_resid with scipy/pingouin-free code (own ranks, own OLS);\\n- recompute held-out AUCs with sklearn roc_auc_score;\\n- they must match to 1e-6;\\n- shuffled-outcome control on held-out: 20 within-group permutations give pooled |psp| < 0.03 on average;\\n- a planted positive control is detected.\\n\\nT8 OUTPUT VALIDATION: aii-json validates method_out.json against exp_gen_sol_out; every file is < 100 MB (aii-file-size-limit); README numbers are cross-checked against rq1_heldout.json by a small script that asserts equality.\",\n  \"domain_practice\": \"WHAT I READ: the run's own prior artifacts on disk (EXP5 README, frozen_spec.json and scan_full.py; EXP3 features.py and config; EXP6 README and deviations; Eval1 F3 portability; the O5 dataset README); art_dxvRpQufMR0e's positioning summary (Guevara 2016 entry AUCs 0.68-0.90; the Weng 2013 precedent; the ANS template); and two targeted searches: Small, Boyack & Klavans 2014 (Research Policy 43:1450-1467), and the forecasting-evaluation literature (the Porter et al. 'Measuring tech emergence: A contest', TFSC 2020; the 2023 'What's next? Forecasting scientific research trends', Heliyon; the 2026 AI phase-transition early-warning preprint arXiv 2606.12828, which freezes thresholds on 2017-21 and scores 2023-25 with precision/recall against a 13.5% base rate). No domain handbook fits scientometrics, so the practice below rests on these sources and the run's measurements.\\n\\nBASELINES. Every emergence-indicator paper reports popularity/growth references: counts, growth, Kleinberg bursts (Kleinberg 2003), and the Porter/Carley emergence score, whose ingredients are novelty, persistence, growth and community. The first comparison a reviewer names is 'does it beat simple growth and volume?'. The 'Measuring tech emergence' contest and Maillart 2026 both show that growth alone explains much of the outcome. In diversification work the reviewer's first rival is relatedness density (Hidalgo 2007; Guevara 2016). Fair tuning means the baseline gets the same window, the same learner and the same CV as the candidate. Here that baseline is B5 (volume, growth, off-home share, entropy, reach) plus log_count/burst/accel/author growth as separate rows, fitted with the same L2 logistic or rank residualisation.\\n\\nDATA. Standard corpora are WoS/Scopus clusters (Small et al. 2014 use co-citation threads of about 14 papers per year), MAG/OpenAlex concepts (the arXiv 2606.03864 link forecasting), and topic-level OpenAlex. Legacy OpenAlex concepts (MAG FoS) are deprecated but still in the snapshot, and they are seeded from Wikipedia, which is a known selection condition. Paper-level topic classifiers used as discipline labels are circular for diffusion, which is why venue labels are used for features.\\n\\nCONTROLS. Hold the feature window fixed and separate it from the outcome window (a gap of 3 or more years). Hold the onset cohort fixed or control for it, because field growth and database coverage change by year. Hold field size fixed through rarefaction or residualisation. The confound most likely to catch this design is VOLUME: a structural indicator that relabels early size. The second is label coverage: Eval1 found all G-variant O1 gains were coverage artefacts.\\n\\nHOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Concept-level forecasting papers use hundreds to thousands of topics. Results with fewer than about 30 units per domain are dismissed (iteration 1's 47-concept panel was underpowered: rho_B5 = 0.83 left no room). Readers expect a CI, not a p-value alone, and per-domain results next to pooled ones. The emerging expectation is a temporal hold-out with frozen thresholds (arXiv 2606.12828). Effects already on record here are small (EXP5 H3 held-out partial rho about 0.03-0.07; EXP3 out-of-group psp for D_ratio 0.34 on 47 concepts, which will shrink). The held-out n here (742/1,113/1,352/165 plus a 4,356-concept cohort) gives an SE of about 0.017 pooled, so a partial rho of about 0.05 is detectable pooled. MATHDEC (SE about 0.08) can only confirm large effects. That is stated up front, not discovered.\\n\\nMEASURES AND REPORTING. For binary outcomes: AUC and delta-AUC over the baseline, or precision@k against the base rate. For continuous outcomes: Spearman or partial correlation. Report both global and per-field results, a random-effects pooled estimate with I2 (standard in this run and in meta-scientometrics), multiplicity correction, and a table of all indicators, not only the winners. Validation against EXTERNAL recognition (MeSH, Wikipedia, curated lists) is the gold standard that Rotolo et al. 2015 and Small et al. 2014 call for. It is rarely done because base rates are low and year-dependent.\",\n  \"practice_alignment\": \"MEETS:\\n(1) Growth/volume baselines are in every comparison: B5 is the conditioning set for every indicator, and popularity rows (count, burst, acceleration, author growth) compete as candidates. The learned models are compared against B5 with the SAME learner.\\n(2) Feature and outcome windows are separated: features use t0..t0+2 only; outcomes use t0+6..t0+8 (O4: t0+3..t0+8). The EXP3 W3 window (t0+3..t0+4) is explicitly cut to avoid leakage.\\n(3) Volume confounding is handled by rarefied O2r, residualised O2r_resid, a DEV size diagnostic per indicator, and partial correlations given B5.\\n(4) Label coverage is a pre-declared sensitivity covariate set (the lesson of Eval1 D).\\n(5) Domain hold-out plus temporal hold-out: 4 held-out home groups, plus a 2010-14 cohort split into DEV-home and non-DEV-home parts. This separates 'new time' from 'new domain'.\\n(6) Per-group results, DL pooling with I2, sign counts, Holm within outcome families, concept-clustered refit bootstrap CIs, and the full portability table for all about 52 indicators. Domain-specific failures are reported, not averaged away, and one is pre-registered (P3).\\n(7) External recognition (O5) is joined for the first time, with at-risk sets and onset-year controls for the Wikipedia wave.\\n(8) The freeze and hash seal before scoring follow the frozen-threshold practice of recent early-warning work.\\n\\nDEPARTURES AND THEIR COST:\\n(a) The held-out outcomes are not virgin. EXP5 already unsealed O1/O3/O2r for these concepts to test H1 (field retention) and H3 (G family). The ~45 other indicators were never scored on them, and no selection here touches held-out rows, so the practical leakage is limited to the G family, whose held-out rows are flagged 'previously scored'. Cost: a reader may call the held-out 'second-use'. It is disclosed in the README and in rq1_heldout.json.\\n(b) Onset t0 = the first year with >= 20 TAG-grounded title matches (the EXP5 frame), not a Porter/Carley emergence score or co-citation threads. Concepts are legacy OpenAlex concepts, which carry a Wikipedia-seeding selection. This is justified because it is the run's single frozen frame, and changing it would break the 'one outcome table, one fold assignment' requirement. Cost: the results hold for named concepts, not for unnamed research fronts.\\n(c) The ego windows are one year each (W1 = t0, W2 = t0+1, W3 = t0+2) instead of EXP3's 2+1+2 years, so they fit the 3-year feature window. Cost: noisier early ego statistics, especially for small concepts. It is mitigated by the T4 check and a DEV-decided threshold fallback, all before the freeze.\\n(d) N_NULL = 200 and a betweenness cutoff, for compute reasons. Cost: small extra noise in D_z and a local, not global, betweenness. Both are logged as deviations.\\n(e) The primary association measure is partial Spearman, not precision@k or AUC, for the continuous outcomes. This matches the direction and the run's iteration-2 practice. precision@top-decile is added to rq1_heldout.json for O2r_m50 and O5_WW so the result can be compared with the forecasting literature. It is cheap and closed inside the plan.\\n(f) No Porter/Carley emergence-score baseline is computed. Its ingredients (novelty = newborn onset, persistence = O1-like, growth, community = author growth) are covered by family E rows, and a composite would add little. Cost: a reviewer may ask for it by name. This is noted as a limitation.\\n(g) Venue labels come from each work's first source, and conference-heavy CS is under-covered (EXP5 deviation: base types article|review only). Cost: DEV CS indicators are measured on a thinner slice, which could make the pre-registered 'CS-only' results partly a coverage artefact. The label-coverage sensitivity addresses this.\\n(h) O5 uses year_usable Wikipedia estimated dates and taxonomy intervals. The dates are coarse, and the Wikipedia creation wave of 2001-07 correlates with t0, so onset-year dummies enter the baseline. Cost: O5 is weaker as ground truth for early-onset concepts. This is reported with base rates by t0.\\n(i) MATHDEC (165 concepts) is underpowered for effects below about 0.2, so its group-level result is reported as uninformative when its CI is wide, not as a failure.\"\n}\n", "structuredPatch": [], "originalFile": null, "userModified": false}

# Plan: which early network signals travel across fields (RQ1 held-out deliverable)

This is the plan-generation step (GEN_PLAN, iteration 3, direction `experiment_iter3_dir2`). No code was run here. The
only deliverable is the structured plan that the experiment executor will follow.

## Layout

| path | content |
|---|---|
| `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json` | the plan: title, summary, builds_on, pseudocode, fallback, testing, domain practice, practice alignment |
| `README.md` | this file |
| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | empty: there are no heavy files in this workspace |

## What the plan does, briefly

- **Frame:** the EXP5 frame (12,499 concepts; the EXP5 split).
- **Snapshot passes:** two zero-credit passes over the OpenAlex S3 snapshot.
  - Pass A: grounded matches with topic and author ids.
  - Pass B: citations to early works.
- **Indicators:** about 52, in 7 families, over t0..t0+2.
- **Outcomes:** one table (O1, O2r, O2r_resid, O3, O4, O5 and O5-WW).
- **Selection and scoring:** a top 10 per outcome is selected on DEV and hash-sealed, then scored once on the held-out
  groups and the cohort.
- **Reporting:** DL pooling, a portability table, pre-registered predictions and learned models (elastic-net/L1, EBM).

## Inputs it reads (run-relative paths)

- `3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_5/`: the frame, outcomes, basic features, scan counts and
  matcher code.
- `3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_3/`: the ego-network features and the topic backbones.
- `3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_6/`: `lib/h2.py`, `stats_core.py` and the phi backbone.
- `3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_evaluation_1/eval_out.json`: F3, the prior for the predictions.
- `3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_2/full_data_out/`: the O5 recognition events (the declared
  dependency).

## Restoring removed files

Nothing is marked `delete`, so there is nothing to restore.

They stay in the matrix, but their held-out rows are labelled 'previously scored', not confirmatory.\",", "   \"implementation_pseudocode\": \"# ======================= 0. SETUP (about 15 min) =======================\\n# Read the skills aii-python, aii-parallel-computing, aii-long-running-tasks, aii-use-hardware and aii-json first.\\n# WS = this workspace; R = the run root. Every write goes under WS.\\nuv venv; uv pip install numpy pandas pyarrow scipy scikit-learn statsmodels python-igraph leidenalg interpret loguru matplotlib pyahocorasick nltk joblib\\n# Copy (do not import across workspaces) into WS/lib/ and record the sha256 of each copy in WS/results/provenance.json:\\n#   EXP5 matcher.py, rangefile.py, common.py (rename to common5.py), lexicon_v1.parquet (check the sha against frozen_lexicon.sha256),\\n#        results/source_field.parquet, snapshot/works_manifest.json\\n#   EXP3 features.py (rename to ego.py), common.py (rename to common3.py), backbone/slice*.npz, scan/topic_ids.json,\\n#        results/topic_meta.csv\\n#   EXP6 lib/h2.py, lib/stats_core.py, inputs/field_backbone.json\\n# If R is not mounted: re-download the manifests with EXP5's restore logic (snapshot/works_manifest.json lists the 2,040 S3\\n#   keys; public, zero credit), rebuild the frame from the definitions, and log everything in WS/results/deviations.json.\\nframe = read_csv(EXP5/frame_concepts.csv)                       # 12,499 rows\\nassert counts by split/group == {DEV 4771, PHYS 742, LIFEENV 1113, SOC 1352, MATHDEC 165, COHORT 4356}\\nframe['cohort_part'] = where(split == COHORT, 'COH_DEVHOME' if group in {CS, Eng, BGM, Med} else 'COH_OTHER', NA)\\nframe['in_exp6'] = concept_id in EXP6/results/frame_concepts.csv   # report the overlap count\\n\\n# ======================= 1. PASS A: grounded frame matches with work/topic/author IDs =======================\\n# One zero-credit pass over all 2,040 files, adapted from EXP5 scan_full.process_file. It runs in the BACKGROUND\\n# (PID-managed, resumable per file) while steps 3-4 are coded.\\nCOLS_A = EXP5.COLS + ['id', 'topics.list.element.id', 'authorships.list.element.author.id']\\n# EXP5.COLS already has title, year, type, is_paratext, is_xpac, source.id, topics field id, primary_topic field id,\\n# concepts id/score. There is no referenced_works here: Pass B handles citations.\\nper file fi:\\n   tb = read_columns(key, size, COLS_A, n_threads=8)\\n   base, vfield, tagstate: computed EXACTLY as in EXP5 (same automaton built from the FULL lexicon_v1, same TAG_MIN = 0.3)\\n   (a) BG[year, topic] += base works per topic per year, and GT[year] += base works with >= 1 topic\\n       (a full-corpus background for 1995-2022; it extends EXP3's ckpt)\\n   (b) h = verified hits with tagstate == 1 AND ci in the frame set.\\n       COUNTS[ci, year, vfield] += 1 for all years  -> used by the reproduction check\\n   (c) for hits with t0[ci] - 3 <= year <= t0[ci] + 2: emit a row\\n       (ci, year, work_id int64, vfield, topic_idx list[int16] via topic_ids.json, author_ids list[int64] only if year >= t0)\\n   (d) RSAMPLE: base works with year 2003..2016 and splitmix64(fi, row) % 400 == 0 (about 0.25%, about 250k works):\\n       emit (work_id, year, vfield). This is the citation reference set for normalising O4.\\n   write the parts to WS/passA/parts/*.parquet and done_{fi}.json\\nmerge -> WS/data/frame_matches_early.parquet (split into parts < 90 MB), bg_topics.npz, counts_check.parquet, ref_sample.parquet\\n# Expected size: about 12.5k concepts x about 150 early works = about 2M rows; topics about 3 ids, authors about 5 ids\\n# -> roughly 100-200 MB of parquet.\\nCHECK A1 (STOP rule): for every frame concept, Spearman over years of COUNTS[ci, year, :].sum() against grounded counts from\\n   EXP5 agg_counts (tagstate == 1). The median must be >= 0.99 AND recomputed early_volume must equal frame.early_volume for\\n   >= 99% of concepts. Otherwise STOP, log the mismatch and diagnose (automaton, lexicon hash, base filter) before any feature.\\nCHECK A2: for the years both cover, BG must reproduce EXP3 ckpt bg with Spearman >= 0.999. Unknown topic ids are counted and logged.\\n\\n# ======================= 2. PASS B: citations received by early works (O4) =======================\\ntargets = sorted int64 array of (early work ids, i.e. year in t0..t0+2 of frame concepts) UNION ref_sample ids\\nCOLS_B = ['publication_year', 'type', 'is_paratext', 'is_xpac', 'referenced_works']\\nper file: citing = base works (same filter) with year 2003..2022; flatten referenced_works -> int64 via utf8_slice at 'W';\\n   m = isin(refs, targets) (np.searchsorted on the sorted targets)\\n   CIT[target_id, citing_year] += 1 (sparse; emit (target_id, citing_year, n))\\nmerge -> WS/data/cites_early.parquet   # EXP6 read referenced_works for all files in 12.7 min\\n# If Pass B has not finished by T+3h15m: drop O4, log it, continue.\\n\\n# ======================= 3. FEATURE MATRIX (window t0..t0+2 ONLY) =======================\\n# Per-concept yearly per-field grounded counts n_cj(t) come from EXP5 agg_counts (tagstate == 1, vfield > 0 = labelled).\\n# home = frame.home (a field code; for intersection-born, parse all home codes the frame records).\\nFAMILY E  (reference / popularity; 'E' rows): log_count, share, growth_ind, accel, burst, logvol, growth_c\\n   (from concept_features_basic)\\n   + author_growth = log(1 + distinct authors t0+2) - log(1 + distinct authors t0)                         (Pass A)\\n   + n_authors_early = log(1 + distinct authors t0..t0+2)\\n   B5 = [logvol, growth_c, offhome_share, entropy, reach]  # identical to iterations 1-2; B5 is the baseline, not a candidate\\nFAMILY F  (disciplinary): lab_entropy, lab_reach, lab_offhome_share, log_offhome_volume (reuse)\\n   + rao_stirling = sum_{i != j} p_i p_j (1 - phi_n[i, j]), with phi_n = phi rescaled to [0, 1]; labelled works t0..t0+2\\n   + fields_gained_per_yr = (|ENTERED(t0+2)| - |ENTERED(t0)|) / 2\\nFAMILY G  (landing): G, G_A, G_btw, G_deg, G_phimin, REL_home, RS (reuse; flag 'previously_scored_heldout')\\nFAMILY FR (retained frontier, D3 verbatim via h2.states() on n_cj(t) restricted to t0..t0+2):\\n   CONTACT_REACH = #off-home fields with >= 1 labelled paper in t0..t0+2\\n   RETAINED_REACH = #off-home fields with >= 2 papers in at least 2 of the 3 years\\n   RETENTION_RATIO_early = RETAINED_REACH / max(CONTACT_REACH, 1)  (NA if CONTACT_REACH = 0 -> 0 plus a missing flag)\\n   FRONTIER_POTENTIAL = sum over k not in ENTERED(t0+2), k != home, of mean_{j in early-retained} phi[j, k]\\n                        (0 if there are no early-retained fields)\\n   also D_rca_end, D_vol_end, M0_density_end at t0+2 (the D3 formulas), kept for the portability table only\\nFAMILY A  (co-occurrence ego network, art_yrradSC27HtQ features.concept_core adapted):\\n   works = [(year, topic_idx tuple)] from frame_matches_early for t0-3..t0+2\\n   windows: PRE = t0-3..t0-1, W1 = {t0}, W2 = {t0+1}, W3 = {t0+2}   # DEVIATION: the EXP3 W3 = t0+3..t0+4 would leak past the window\\n   new_edge_rate = (M / 3) / (n1 + 1)                                # the window is 3 years, not 5\\n   background: bg_window() from Pass A's BG/GT (not EXP3's ckpt, whose year range may stop early)\\n   SELF topics: the EXP3 rule, with the concept name plus aliases_used from the frame\\n   slice_of(): the EXP3 SLICES (2000-04, 05-09, 10-14); years 2015-16 clamp to slice 2 (log it)\\n   outputs kept (about 26): D_z, D_ratio, D_rare, D_sub, D_obs(=M new neighbours), NOV, NOV_res, F_res, F_z,\\n     deg_W1, deg_W3, deg_growth, str_growth, new_edge_rate, edge_persistence, turnover, participation, n_comm_W3,\\n     comm_entropy (Shannon entropy of W3 neighbour-community weights; new, 3 lines), comm_transitions,\\n     ego_density_W3, ego_density_change, btw_t4 (rename btw_end), btw_change, kcore_end, constraint_end, constraint_change\\n   dropped as near-duplicate variants: D_lag, D_q, D_withself, F_bg (logged)\\n   SPEED: N_NULL = 200 (EXP3 used 200 for its reliability splits); betweenness with cutoff = 4 on the kNN backbone.\\n   Timing test on 60 random concepts first (see testing_plan); extrapolate over 7 workers x 4 vCPU.\\n   If the projection exceeds 100 min: keep ALL held-out-group concepts (3,372) and a stratified random 2,000 DEV plus\\n   2,000 COHORT (seed 20260928, stratified by group x t0), and log it.\\n   Parallelism: ProcessPoolExecutor(spawn, 4 workers), with the context loaded once per worker as in EXP3.\\nFAMILY S  (social; candidate S, Cheng-style): for off-home labelled early works (vfield not home, t0..t0+2):\\n   build a graph with authors as nodes and a clique per paper; S_comp = #connected components / #off-home papers;\\n   S_comp_n = #components / #distinct off-home authors; also S_isolated_share.\\n   If author ids are missing for > 50% of those works, compute S only where coverage >= 50% and log coverage.\\n   If the id column is unreadable, drop family S and log it.\\nOUTPUT: WS/results/indicator_matrix.parquet (ci x about 52 indicators + family tags) and indicator_dictionary.csv\\n   (name, family, window, formula, source file, expected sign from F3 where it exists, pre-registered prediction flag).\\nDIAGNOSTICS on DEV only: missing rates; Spearman of each indicator with logvol and growth_c (the size diagnostic,\\n   flag |rho| > 0.6); the indicator x indicator Spearman matrix with average-linkage clustering on 1 - |rho|\\n   -> results/indicator_corr_dev.csv and figures/indicator_clusters.png.\\n\\n# ======================= 4. OUTCOMES (one table, one fold assignment) =======================\\nO1c  = log(1 + N_grounded t0+6..t0+8) - log(1 + N_grounded t0..t0+2)   (continuous; the direction's definition; from agg_counts)\\nO1b  = concept_outcomes.O1 (EXP5 binary sustained-share rule; secondary, AUC)\\nO2r_m50 (primary breadth), O2r_m30 = concept_outcomes (exact hypergeometric, no truncation)\\nO2r_resid = O2r_m50 - (a + b * logvol), with a and b fitted by OLS on DEV ONLY, frozen and reported next to EXP5's constants\\nO3   = concept_outcomes.O3 (the art_33 transience rule verbatim; report base rates per group)\\nO4   = log((1 + C_late / 6) / (1 + C_early / 3)) summed over the concept's early works, where C_early = citations in\\n       t0..t0+2 and C_late = citations in t0+3..t0+8 (cites_early),\\n       MINUS the same quantity for the ref_sample works matched on (publication year, venue field), taking the\\n       expectation over the concept's early-work mix of year x field. This is field-and-year normalised\\n       (an MNCS-style expected value).\\nO5   (art_O7Dq4L02QnDN): join on concept_id == int(openalex_id.strip('C')); fall back to qid_resolved; report the join rate.\\n     Events used: year_usable == True AND relation == 'same', from the sources MeSH (DateIntroduced), Wikipedia creation\\n     (exact or calibrated estimate), Wikidata P571/P575, taxonomy_added_between (ACM CCS, MSC, PACS/PhySH), and curated\\n     lists EXCEPT Clarivate/CAS Research Fronts, which are citation-derived and circular with O4.\\n     AT RISK: no qualifying event with year < t0 and no mesh_baseline flag. Concepts not at risk are set to NA for O5.\\n     O5 = 1 if any qualifying event has t0 <= year <= t0+8 (MeSH and taxonomy require year > t0).\\n     O5_WW = the same, using Wikipedia and Wikidata only; defined for every group.\\n     Report the base rate and the at-risk n per group. A group with < 20 positives is DROPPED from O5 scoring (O5_WW too).\\n     Because the Wikipedia creation wave is 2001-07, the O5/O5_WW baselines also include onset-year dummies (B5+t0).\\n     Sensitivity: relation in {same, narrower}.\\nOUTCOME SEAL: write WS/data/outcomes_dev.parquet (DEV rows) and WS/data/outcomes_sealed.parquet (HELDOUT + COHORT rows).\\n   Log the sha256 of the sealed file. lib/seal.py load_heldout() raises unless results/frozen_spec.json exists and its\\n   hash matches logs/seal.log, and it refuses a second call (the EXP5 seal.py pattern).\\n   NOTE in the README: EXP5 already unsealed O1/O3/O2r for these concepts for its H1/H3 tests. Held-out is fresh for\\n   every indicator EXCEPT the G family.\\n\\n# ======================= 5. DEV SELECTION (DEV rows only) =======================\\nContinuous outcomes {O1c, O2r_m50, O2r_resid, O4}:\\n   psp(x, y | B5) = Pearson( resid(rank x ~ rank B5 + group dummies + t0 dummies), resid(rank y ~ same) )\\n   CI: 1,000 concept-bootstrap resamples; the residualisation is REFITTED in each resample (the concept is the unit).\\nBinary outcomes {O1b, O3, O5, O5_WW}:\\n   dAUC = AUC(B5 + x) - AUC(B5), for L2 logistic models evaluated leave-one-DEV-group-out (4 folds by home group) with\\n   out-of-fold predictions; x is standardised on the training folds. CI: 1,000 concept-bootstrap resamples refitting both\\n   models (a vectorised Newton-IRLS from EXP5 models.py).\\nPer outcome, frozen ranking rule:\\n   eligible = indicators with DEV CI excluding 0 and missing <= 30%\\n   order by |estimate| descending; greedy de-duplication: skip x if |Spearman(x, already selected)| > 0.85 on DEV\\n   top10[outcome] = the first 10; if fewer than 10 are eligible, fill by |estimate| (flag 'filled')\\n   store the frozen SIGN of each selected indicator\\nunion_top10 = the 10 indicators with the best mean rank across the 8 outcomes (same de-duplication)\\nSensitivity rankings (reported, not used to freeze): given B5 + label_coverage_early + tag_coverage + precision_c;\\n   and ranking by AUC of top vs bottom tercile within group.\\nLEARNED MODELS (all DEV, hyperparameters by leave-one-DEV-group-out CV):\\n   continuous: ElasticNetCV(l1_ratio in {0.1, 0.5, 0.9, 1.0}) on standardised indicators + B5;\\n     ExplainableBoostingRegressor(interactions=10, outer_bags=8, random_state=20260928)\\n   binary: LogisticRegressionCV(penalty='l1', solver='saga', Cs=20);\\n     ExplainableBoostingClassifier(interactions=10, outer_bags=8)\\n   imputation: DEV medians plus missing flags for indicators with > 5% missing; the scaler is frozen\\n   saved: learned_model.json (coefficients, the L1 path over C, EBM term importances, shape-function tables for the\\n     top 8 terms, pairwise terms), models/*.joblib\\nPOWER (DEV simulation): plant partial rho = 0.05 / 0.10 in the held-out group sizes; report P(CI > 0) per group and pooled.\\n   Expected SE of partial rho: about 0.037 (PHYS), 0.03 (LIFEENV), 0.027 (SOC), 0.078 (MATHDEC), 0.017 (pooled).\\n   Store these as MDE = 2.8 x SE in frozen_spec.\\nFREEZE: results/frozen_spec.json = {indicator list and formulas, window definitions, N_NULL, B5, covariate sets,\\n   the O2r_resid a/b, the O5 rules, top10 per outcome with signs, union_top10, learner hyperparameters, scaler constants,\\n   imputation medians, bootstrap B and seed, Holm families, DL method, pre-registered predictions P1-P5 (below),\\n   sha256 of every lib/*.py and of indicator_matrix.parquet}\\n   -> write sha256(frozen_spec.json) to logs/seal.log; git-commit the code; ONLY THEN call load_heldout() (once).\\n\\n# ======================= 6. HELD-OUT SCORING (once) =======================\\nunits = [PHYS, LIFEENV, SOC, MATHDEC, COH_DEVHOME, COH_OTHER]\\nfor each outcome, for each indicator in top10[outcome] (and union_top10), for each unit:\\n   continuous: psp given B5 + t0 dummies within the unit; concept bootstrap, B = 1,000\\n   binary: dAUC using the FROZEN DEV coefficients (B5 model and B5 + x model) applied to the unit. CI: joint refit\\n     bootstrap (resample DEV concepts -> refit -> resample unit concepts -> score), B = 1,000\\n   DL-pool the 4 held-out groups (Fisher z for rho; dAUC with bootstrap SE) -> pooled estimate, 95% CI, tau2, I2\\n   sign agreement with the frozen sign: count over 4 groups + 2 cohort parts; exact sign test p\\nHolm within each outcome family (the 10 pooled tests of that outcome's top 10), using p from the pooled z.\\nLEARNED vs SINGLE vs B5 on the SAME held-out units:\\n   continuous: Spearman(pred, y) and out-of-sample R2 of B5-only ElasticNet vs ElasticNet-all vs EBM vs B5 + best single\\n     (the best single is the DEV #1 indicator, frozen); paired concept bootstrap for the differences\\n   binary: AUC, delta-AUC, Brier score and calibration slope for the same four models\\nPORTABILITY TABLE (all about 52 indicators, x 4 DEV groups + 4 held-out groups + 2 cohort parts): psp with O2r_m50, O2r_resid\\n   and O1c, plus the raw Spearman with O2r_m50. Rows outside the top 10 are labelled EXPLORATORY.\\n   -> portability_table.csv (indicator, family, unit, outcome, n, rho, ci_lo, ci_hi, frozen/exploratory,\\n      previously_scored flag)\\nPRE-REGISTERED PREDICTIONS (frozen before the unseal; evaluated on the pooled held-out groups):\\n   P1 entropy, D_rare, D_ratio, participation and NOV_res: raw Spearman with O2r_m50 > 0 (CI > 0) in >= 3 of 4 groups;\\n      AND for D_rare, D_ratio, participation and NOV_res: pooled psp | B5 CI upper bound < 0.10 ('add little').\\n      Entropy is in B5, so only its raw part is testable.\\n   P2 edge_persistence: pooled raw rho and psp with O2r_m50 both < 0.\\n   P3 deg_growth, str_growth, new_edge_rate: FAIL held-out = pooled psp CI includes 0 OR a sign flip in >= 2 of 4 groups.\\n      Also report their CS-only DEV values.\\n   P4 RETENTION_RATIO_early and FRONTIER_POTENTIAL: pooled psp > 0 with CI > 0 for O2r_resid AND O1c.\\n   P5 CONTACT_REACH: pooled psp CI includes 0. Because B5 contains reach, ALSO report P4/P5 given B5-minus-reach, so the\\n      test is not trivial by construction.\\n   Each prediction gets a verdict (HOLDS / FAILS / UNTESTABLE) in rq1_heldout.json.\\nSENSITIVITIES (post-seal, labelled): excluding in_exp6 concepts; excluding intersection-born; label-coverage covariates\\n   added; O5 relation in {same, narrower}; O2r_m30.\\n\\n# ======================= 7. EXPLAIN, FIGURES, OUTPUTS =======================\\nfigures/portability_heatmap.png (+pdf): indicator (rows, grouped by family) x unit; colour = psp with O2r_m50; a dot where\\n   the CI excludes 0; frozen top-10 rows in bold\\nfigures/heldout_forest_<outcome>.png: per-unit and DL-pooled estimates with CIs for the top 10\\nfigures/learned_vs_single.png: bars of held-out delta-AUC / delta-Spearman with CIs for the models, the best single and B5\\nfigures/ebm_shapes.png: the top 6 EBM shape functions for O2r_resid and O5_WW\\nfigures/indicator_clusters.png (DEV correlation dendrogram)\\nfigures/o5_base_rates.png\\nCase exemplars: for the best portable indicator, list the 3 held-out concepts with the highest and the lowest predicted\\n   O2r_resid, with their top-10 W3 ego neighbours (features.py _top_nb_W3) -> results/case_exemplars.json\\nWrite: indicator_matrix.parquet, outcomes.parquet (merged after the unseal), rq1_dev_selection.json, frozen_spec.json\\n   (+ logs/seal.log), rq1_heldout.json, portability_table.csv, learned_model.json, deviations.json, provenance.json,\\n   method_out.json (exp_gen_sol_out: one example per concept with input = the indicator vector, output = outcomes,\\n   predict_B5 / predict_best_single / predict_EBM for every outcome; DEV rows are out-of-fold)\\n   + full/mini/preview variants (aii-json; split with aii-file-size-limit if > limit).\\nREADME.md (layout, how to run, results table, 'Restoring removed files') and .aii/manifest.yaml:\\n   keep: data/frame_matches_early*.parquet, cites_early.parquet, bg_topics.npz, results/, figures/, models/;\\n   delete: .venv/ (regenerable: uv venv + uv pip install -r requirements.txt); passA/parts/ and passB/parts/\\n   (regenerable: uv run passA.py / passB.py); __pycache__/.\",", "   \"fallback_plan\": \"F1 SNAPSHOT UNREACHABLE OR TOO SLOW for Pass A (projected > 90 min after 40 files):\\n(a) First reduce COLS_A: drop author ids, which drops family S (log it).\\n(b) If still too slow, process a deterministic 50% of files (every even file index). This is a random subsample of works, so ego counts are halved. Mark it in deviations and keep the EXP5 agg_counts reproduction check on the sampled files only (expected ratio about 0.5).\\n(c) If the S3 snapshot is entirely unreachable: family A cannot be computed. Deliver the RQ1 matrix from families E, F, G and FR and B5 (about 26 indicators, all computable from agg_counts and concept_features_basic). Add a coarse FIELD-level co-occurrence ego proxy from agg_counts ptfield (the primary-topic field of each grounded work) against the 26-field co_by_year background: new-field-neighbour count, persistence, participation over the phi-Leiden communities of the 26-field backbone. State plainly that the fine topic-level ego network was not recomputed.\\n\\nF2 CHECK A1 FAILS (median Spearman < 0.99 against agg_counts). Diagnose in this order: lexicon sha, automaton built from the full lexicon, base-type filter, TAG_MIN, venue lookup. Fix and rerun on 5 files before continuing. Never proceed with an unreproduced frame.\\n\\nF3 PASS B DOES NOT FIT (not finished by T+3h15m, or targets > 5M ids): drop O4 and log it. As a secondary substitute, use the snapshot's present-day cited_by_count of early works, normalised by the ref-sample year x field mean. That column is cheap and could be added to Pass A as 'cited_by_count'; mark it as NOT windowed. It has no role in selection. If both are missing, O4 is dropped from all tables.\\n\\nF4 EGO FEATURES TOO SLOW (timing test > 100 min projected): apply in order\\n(i) N_NULL 200 -> 100;\\n(ii) betweenness cutoff 4 -> 3, or replace it with the degree of the inserted node in the kNN graph;\\n(iii) the stratified subsample (all held-out concepts, 2,000 DEV, 2,000 COHORT).\\nEach step is logged; held-out groups are never subsampled.\\n\\nF5 O5 SPARSE: if fewer than 2 held-out groups have >= 20 positives for O5, the taxonomy/MeSH O5 is reported descriptively only, and O5_WW becomes the only confirmatory recognition outcome. If O5_WW is also < 20 in a group, that group is dropped from O5 scoring and the base rates are reported. If the join rate is < 90%, try the qid_resolved join, then report the unmatched concepts.\\n\\nF6 DEGENERATE BINARY OUTCOMES: O3 or O1b with < 20 positives in a group means that group is dropped for that outcome (O3 was all-Medicine in iteration 1; check the base rates first).\\n\\nF7 EBM / interpret INSTALL FAILS: use sklearn HistGradientBoosting with monotone-free settings plus permutation importance and 1-D partial dependence as the interpretable substitute, and log it.\\n\\nF8 TIME PRESSURE at T+4h45m: skip the sensitivities and case exemplars. The sealed held-out scoring of the top 10, the learned-vs-single comparison, the portability table, the prediction verdicts, the README and the manifest are mandatory and come first.\\n\\nF9 AN INDICATOR IS ALL-NaN IN A HELD-OUT GROUP (e.g. ego features need M >= 3): report n and mark it UNTESTABLE for that group. Never impute outcomes, and never impute features on held-out with anything but the frozen DEV medians.\",", "-  \"testing_plan\": \"T0 UNIT TESTS (no network, < 5 min; results/unit_tests.json).\\n(1) h2.states() on a hand-made 5-field x 6-year count matrix: ENTERED/RETAINED/LOST are correct, including the '>= 2 years earlier' retained rule and the off-home restriction.\\n(2) RETAINED_REACH and FRONTIER_POTENTIAL on a toy phi (3 fields) against hand values.\\n(3) Partial Spearman equals the Pearson of rank residuals; on data where y = f(B5) + noise with x independent, the mean psp over 200 simulations is within 0.01 of 0.\\n(4) The dAUC pipeline with a planted effect (x adds 0.5 SD log-odds) returns dAUC > 0.02; with shuffled x, |mean| < 0.005.\\n(5) The DL pooling routine reproduces a textbook example (EXP6 stats_core, or metafor's dat.bcg log RR: tau2 = 0.313).\\n(6) The seal gate raises if frozen_spec is missing and raises on a second unseal.\\n(7) The O5 rule on 4 synthetic concepts: an event before t0 makes the concept not at risk; t0+8 is inclusive; Research Fronts are ignored.\\n(8) Ego features on EXP3's P78 dev concepts with the ORIGINAL EXP3 windows reproduce EXP3 results/features*.csv (D_ratio, participation, NOV_res; Spearman >= 0.95 after title-match -> TAG differences). This validates the port before the window change.\\n\\nT1 PASS A MINI (3 files: take indices 65, 1125 and 1407, which EXP5 used for its stage tests).\\n- Runtime per file gives an extrapolation over 2,040 files at 4 workers. Proceed if <= 70 min, else fallback F1(a).\\n- The per-file hit counts for frame concepts with tagstate == 1 must EXACTLY equal the counts implied by EXP5 scan/stage_test_parts/agg_{fi}.npz (decode uC keys: ci, year, vfield, ptfield, tagstate, mt).\\n- Topic id mapping: < 1% unknown ids.\\n\\nT2 PASS A FULL: check A1 (median Spearman >= 0.99; early_volume equal for >= 99%) and check A2 (background Spearman >= 0.999 against EXP3 ckpt). Report the numbers in results/checks.json.\\n\\nT3 PASS B MINI (3 files): some early works receive citations; citing years are >= the cited year for > 99.5% of links; the per-file runtime extrapolates to <= 25 min.\\n\\nT4 EGO TIMING AND SANITY on 60 random DEV concepts:\\n- time per concept, projected to the full frame;\\n- no all-NaN column;\\n- M (new neighbours) has median > 3; if the median is <= 3 with 1-year windows, switch to W1 = t0..t0+1, W2 = t0+1..t0+2 is NOT allowed (overlap).
