The number of indicators with DEV CI excluding 0 must be about 5% of about 52 (<= 6 on average); otherwise the bootstrap is anticonservative, so fix it before freezing.\\n- Learned-model LOGO-CV on DEV beats B5 by a stated margin, or this is reported as a null.\\n\\nT6 PRE-UNSEAL CHECKLIST (logs/seal.log):\\n- no held-out/cohort outcome column is present in any object built before the freeze (grep the pickles/parquets written before the freeze for the sealed column names on held-out ci);\\n- git commit hash;\\n- frozen_spec sha.\\n\\nT7 POST-SCORING AUDIT (independent code path, audit.py):\\n- recompute the pooled psp for the top 3 indicators of O2r_resid with scipy/pingouin-free code (own ranks, own OLS);\\n- recompute held-out AUCs with sklearn roc_auc_score;\\n- they must match to 1e-6;\\n- shuffled-outcome control on held-out: 20 within-group permutations give pooled |psp| < 0.03 on average;\\n- a planted positive control is detected.\\n\\nT8 OUTPUT VALIDATION: aii-json validates method_out.json against exp_gen_sol_out; every file is < 100 MB (aii-file-size-limit); README numbers are cross-checked against rq1_heldout.json by a small script that asserts equality.\",", "   \"domain_practice\": \"WHAT I READ: the run's own prior artifacts on disk (EXP5 README, frozen_spec.json and scan_full.py; EXP3 features.py and config; EXP6 README and deviations; Eval1 F3 portability; the O5 dataset README); art_dxvRpQufMR0e's positioning summary (Guevara 2016 entry AUCs 0.68-0.90; the Weng 2013 precedent; the ANS template); and two targeted searches: Small, Boyack & Klavans 2014 (Research Policy 43:1450-1467), and the forecasting-evaluation literature (the Porter et al. 'Measuring tech emergence: A contest', TFSC 2020; the 2023 'What's next? Forecasting scientific research trends', Heliyon; the 2026 AI phase-transition early-warning preprint arXiv 2606.12828, which freezes thresholds on 2017-21 and scores 2023-25 with precision/recall against a 13.5% base rate). No domain handbook fits scientometrics, so the practice below rests on these sources and the run's measurements.\\n\\nBASELINES. Every emergence-indicator paper reports popularity/growth references: counts, growth, Kleinberg bursts (Kleinberg 2003), and the Porter/Carley emergence score, whose ingredients are novelty, persistence, growth and community. The first comparison a reviewer names is 'does it beat simple growth and volume?'. The 'Measuring tech emergence' contest and Maillart 2026 both show that growth alone explains much of the outcome. In diversification work the reviewer's first rival is relatedness density (Hidalgo 2007; Guevara 2016). Fair tuning means the baseline gets the same window, the same learner and the same CV as the candidate. Here that baseline is B5 (volume, growth, off-home share, entropy, reach) plus log_count/burst/accel/author growth as separate rows, fitted with the same L2 logistic or rank residualisation.\\n\\nDATA. Standard corpora are WoS/Scopus clusters (Small et al. 2014 use co-citation threads of about 14 papers per year), MAG/OpenAlex concepts (the arXiv 2606.03864 link forecasting), and topic-level OpenAlex. Legacy OpenAlex concepts (MAG FoS) are deprecated but still in the snapshot, and they are seeded from Wikipedia, which is a known selection condition. Paper-level topic classifiers used as discipline labels are circular for diffusion, which is why venue labels are used for features.\\n\\nCONTROLS. Hold the feature window fixed and separate it from the outcome window (a gap of 3 or more years). Hold the onset cohort fixed or control for it, because field growth and database coverage change by year. Hold field size fixed through rarefaction or residualisation. The confound most likely to catch this design is VOLUME: a structural indicator that relabels early size. The second is label coverage: Eval1 found all G-variant O1 gains were coverage artefacts.\\n\\nHOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Concept-level forecasting papers use hundreds to thousands of topics. Results with fewer than about 30 units per domain are dismissed (iteration 1's 47-concept panel was underpowered: rho_B5 = 0.83 left no room). Readers expect a CI, not a p-value alone, and per-domain results next to pooled ones. The emerging expectation is a temporal hold-out with frozen thresholds (arXiv 2606.12828). Effects already on record here are small (EXP5 H3 held-out partial rho about 0.03-0.07; EXP3 out-of-group psp for D_ratio 0.34 on 47 concepts, which will shrink). The held-out n here (742/1,113/1,352/165 plus a 4,356-concept cohort) gives an SE of about 0.017 pooled, so a partial rho of about 0.05 is detectable pooled. MATHDEC (SE about 0.08) can only confirm large effects. That is stated up front, not discovered.\\n\\nMEASURES AND REPORTING. For binary outcomes: AUC and delta-AUC over the baseline, or precision@k against the base rate. For continuous outcomes: Spearman or partial correlation. Report both global and per-field results, a random-effects pooled estimate with I2 (standard in this run and in meta-scientometrics), multiplicity correction, and a table of all indicators, not only the winners. Validation against EXTERNAL recognition (MeSH, Wikipedia, curated lists) is the gold standard that Rotolo et al. 2015 and Small et al. 2014 call for. It is rarely done because base rates are low and year-dependent.\",", "   \"practice_alignment\": \"MEETS:\\n(1) Growth/volume baselines are in every comparison: B5 is the conditioning set for every indicator, and popularity rows (count, burst, acceleration, author growth) compete as candidates. The learned models are compared against B5 with the SAME learner.\\n(2) Feature and outcome windows are separated: features use t0..t0+2 only; outcomes use t0+6..t0+8 (O4: t0+3..t0+8). The EXP3 W3 window (t0+3..t0+4) is explicitly cut to avoid leakage.\\n(3) Volume confounding is handled by rarefied O2r, residualised O2r_resid, a DEV size diagnostic per indicator, and partial correlations given B5.\\n(4) Label coverage is a pre-declared sensitivity covariate set (the lesson of Eval1 D).\\n(5) Domain hold-out plus temporal hold-out: 4 held-out home groups, plus a 2010-14 cohort split into DEV-home and non-DEV-home parts. This separates 'new time' from 'new domain'.\\n(6) Per-group results, DL pooling with I2, sign counts, Holm within outcome families, concept-clustered refit bootstrap CIs, and the full portability table for all about 52 indicators. Domain-specific failures are reported, not averaged away, and one is pre-registered (P3).\\n(7) External recognition (O5) is joined for the first time, with at-risk sets and onset-year controls for the Wikipedia wave.\\n(8) The freeze and hash seal before scoring follow the frozen-threshold practice of recent early-warning work.\\n\\nDEPARTURES AND THEIR COST:\\n(a) The held-out outcomes are not virgin. EXP5 already unsealed O1/O3/O2r for these concepts to test H1 (field retention) and H3 (G family). The ~45 other indicators were never scored on them, and no selection here touches held-out rows, so the practical leakage is limited to the G family, whose held-out rows are flagged 'previously scored'. Cost: a reader may call the held-out 'second-use'. It is disclosed in the README and in rq1_heldout.json.\\n(b) Onset t0 = the first year with >= 20 TAG-grounded title matches (the EXP5 frame), not a Porter/Carley emergence score or co-citation threads. Concepts are legacy OpenAlex concepts, which carry a Wikipedia-seeding selection. This is justified because it is the run's single frozen frame, and changing it would break the 'one outcome table, one fold assignment' requirement. Cost: the results hold for named concepts, not for unnamed research fronts.\\n(c) The ego windows are one year each (W1 = t0, W2 = t0+1, W3 = t0+2) instead of EXP3's 2+1+2 years, so they fit the 3-year feature window. Cost: noisier early ego statistics, especially for small concepts. It is mitigated by the T4 check and a DEV-decided threshold fallback, all before the freeze.\\n(d) N_NULL = 200 and a betweenness cutoff, for compute reasons. Cost: small extra noise in D_z and a local, not global, betweenness. Both are logged as deviations.\\n(e) The primary association measure is partial Spearman, not precision@k or AUC, for the continuous outcomes. This matches the direction and the run's iteration-2 practice. precision@top-decile is added to rq1_heldout.json for O2r_m50 and O5_WW so the result can be compared with the forecasting literature. It is cheap and closed inside the plan.\\n(f) No Porter/Carley emergence-score baseline is computed. Its ingredients (novelty = newborn onset, persistence = O1-like, growth, community = author growth) are covered by family E rows, and a composite would add little. Cost: a reviewer may ask for it by name. This is noted as a limitation.\\n(g) Venue labels come from each work's first source, and conference-heavy CS is under-covered (EXP5 deviation: base types article|review only). Cost: DEV CS indicators are measured on a thinner slice, which could make the pre-registered 'CS-only' results partly a coverage artefact. The label-coverage sensitivity addresses this.\\n(h) O5 uses year_usable Wikipedia estimated dates and taxonomy intervals. The dates are coarse, and the Wikipedia creation wave of 2001-07 correlates with t0, so onset-year dummies enter the baseline. Cost: O5 is weaker as ground truth for early-onset concepts. This is reported with base rates by t0.\\n(i) MATHDEC (165 concepts) is underpowered for effects below about 0.2, so its group-level result is reported as uninformative when its CI is wide, not as a failure.\"", " }"]}], "userModified": false, "replaceAll": false}

### [67] ASSISTANT · 2026-09-28 21:28:12 UTC

```
I've written the plan for the held-out test of early network indicators to `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`, with a short `README.md` and an empty `.aii/manifest.yaml` (this workspace holds no large files). Nothing was run: this step only plans. The plan is set up to spend $0 of OpenRouter, use 0 OpenAlex credits, and take about 5 hours on `cpu_plus`.

**What the plan does:**
- **Data:** it keeps the EXP5 set of 12,499 concepts and its existing split into development, held-out and 2010–14 cohort concepts. It adds two free passes over the OpenAlex S3 snapshot:
  - Pass A reruns EXP5's matcher unchanged and keeps work, topic and author IDs for these concepts. It must reproduce EXP5's yearly counts (Spearman ≥ 0.99) before anything else runs.
  - Pass B counts citations to each concept's early papers (outcome O4). EXP6 read the same heavy columns for all 2,040 files in 12.7 minutes, so this should fit.
- **Indicators:** about 52, all computed from the first three years (t0..t0+2) only, in 7 declared families. The ~26 topic co-occurrence network indicators reuse EXP3's `features.py` and its topic backbones. The others are field reach and entropy, the gateway variants, 4 "retained frontier" rows, simple count baselines plus B5, and candidate S (unconnected co-author groups).
- **Outcomes:** one table with O1 (continuous and binary), O2r at m = 30 and 50, O2r_resid, O3, O4 (normalised by year and field against a random reference sample of papers), and O5 external recognition plus a Wikipedia/Wikidata-only variant. O5 counts only concepts not already recognised before onset, and adds onset-year controls because of Wikipedia's 2001–07 creation wave.
- **Selection and scoring:** indicators are ranked on development concepts only, by partial Spearman given B5 or by AUC gain over B5, with concept-clustered bootstrap CIs. Near-duplicate indicators are skipped. A top 10 per outcome, a union top 10, an elastic-net/L1 model and an EBM are then hash-sealed and scored once on each held-out group and each cohort part.
- **Reporting:** pooled estimates with I², Holm correction within each outcome, a portability table covering all ~52 indicators, and a verdict for each of the five pre-registered predictions.

**Problems in the direction that the plan resolves:**
- **Held-out outcomes already opened:** EXP5 already unsealed the O1, O3 and O2r outcomes for these held-out concepts when it tested the gateway indicators. The held-out test is still fresh for every indicator except that gateway family, and the plan labels those rows "previously scored" rather than confirmatory.
- **Leaky time windows:** EXP3's ego-network windows reached t0+3..t0+4, past the three-year feature window. The plan shortens them to one year each (t0, t0+1, t0+2) and logs this as a deviation. The noisier early estimates this causes are checked on development concepts before sealing.
- **Predictions that were true by construction:** B5 already contains entropy and reach. So the entropy and CONTACT_REACH predictions are also tested against B5 without reach, or on raw association only.
- **Circular recognition source:** Research Fronts are excluded from O5 because they are built from citations, which would make them circular with O4.

**Power:** pooled across held-out groups, a partial correlation of about 0.05 is detectable. MATHDEC has only 165 concepts, so it can confirm only large effects, and the plan says so up front.

**Fallbacks:** each has a logged fallback — the snapshot being slow or unreachable, the count check failing, O4 not fitting in time, too few O5 positives, slow ego features, and the EBM package failing to install.

The literature anchors came from two quick searches: [Small, Boyack & Klavans 2014](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/Identifying-emerging-topics-in-science-and-Small-Boyack/ae4329efd3f2441a6678a23ad495c08cf425e96c), the [Measuring tech emergence contest](https://ideas.repec.org/a/eee/tefoso/v159y2020ics0040162520310027.html), the [early-warning preprint with frozen thresholds](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.12828), and [What's next? Forecasting scientific research trends](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2405844023109893).
```
